====================================================================
2. AUTHENTICATION FLOW (GOOGLE OAUTH2)
====================================================================
Implementation Summary:
- Local app login is required first via `/api/local/login` in `server.js`.
- After local login, Google OAuth begins at `/auth/google`.
- OAuth2 client is created by `buildOAuthClient()` using env vars:
  - GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID = [HIDDEN_CREDENTIALS]
  - GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET = [HIDDEN_CREDENTIALS]
  - GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI (or computed fallback)
- State protection is used:
  - `req.session.oauthState` is generated and validated in `/oauth2callback`.
- On callback (`/oauth2callback`):
  - `oauthClient.getToken({ code, redirect_uri })` exchanges auth code for tokens.
  - Tokens are stored in session memory: `req.session.tokens`.
  - Profile is fetched via Google OAuth2 userinfo and saved in `req.session.user`.
  - Granted scopes are checked with `getTokenInfo(access_token)` and stored in `req.session.grantedScopes`.

Token/Session Handling Locations:
- `server.js`
  - `getOAuthClientForRequest(req)` reads `req.session.tokens` and sets OAuth client credentials.
  - `/oauth2callback` writes `req.session.tokens`.
  - `/auth/logout` and `/api/local/logout` clear token/session fields.
- Storage model:
  - OAuth tokens are NOT persisted in MongoDB.
  - Tokens are only in server-side Express session store (default memory store unless overridden).

Files involved:
- `server.js` (all OAuth + session logic)
- `.env` (Google/Mongo/session configuration; values masked)
- `client/src/pages/LoginPage.jsx` and dashboard/UI routes trigger auth actions

Security/Operational Notes:
- Because token storage is session-memory based, server restart drops active Google sessions.
- Session store defaults to in-memory express-session store (not production-grade persistence).

====================================================================
3. DATABASE SCHEMA (MONGODB)
====================================================================
No Mongoose models found.
The project uses native MongoDB driver (`mongodb` package) and implicit document schemas in `server.js`.

Database/Collections:
- DB name resolves from `MONGODB_DB_NAME` or URI path.
- Collections created/used:
  1) `event_snapshots`
  2) `calendar_events`
  3) `user_profiles`
  4) `users`

Indexes (created at startup):
- `event_snapshots`: unique index `{ email: 1 }`
- `calendar_events`: unique index `{ email: 1, eventId: 1 }`
- `calendar_events`: index `{ email: 1, googleUpdatedAt: -1 }`
- `user_profiles`: unique index `{ email: 1 }`
- `users`: unique index `{ username: 1 }`

Inferred document schema: `calendar_events`
- email: String
- eventId: String
- calendarId: String
- status: String|null
- summary: String|null
- description: String|null
- location: String|null
- htmlLink: String|null
- creatorEmail: String|null
- organizerEmail: String|null
- start: String|Date-like
- end: String|Date-like
- googleUpdatedAt: Date|null
- queryMeta: Object
- rawEvent: Object (full Google event payload)
- lastSyncedAt: Date
- deletedAt: Date|null

Inferred document schema: `event_snapshots`
- email: String (unique)
- payload: Object (fetchedAt, calendarId, timeMin, maxResults, count, events[])
- queryMeta: Object
- updatedAt: Date

Inferred document schema: `user_profiles`
- email: String (unique)
- name: String|null
- picture: String|null
- grantedScopes: String[]
- lastLoginAt: Date

Inferred document schema: `users`
- username: String (unique)
- password.salt: String
- password.hash: String
- createdAt: Date
- lastLoginAt: Date|null

====================================================================
4. DEPENDENCIES
====================================================================
Root package.json dependencies:
- dotenv ^17.2.4
- express ^5.2.1
- express-session ^1.19.0

====================================================================
6. CURRENT BUGS / GAPS (CHATBOT READINESS)
====================================================================
1) Chatbot does not execute calendar write actions from natural language.
- `/api/chat` in `routes/chat.js` only searches and replies from stored events.
- No intent-to-action path for "create", "modify", or "delete" through chat messages.

2) OAuth token persistence is session-only.
- Tokens are stored in `req.session.tokens` only.
- After server restart/session loss, user must reconnect Google.
- This limits continuity for production chatbot usage.

3) Session storage is default MemoryStore.
- `express-session` is configured without Redis/Mongo-backed session store.
- Not scalable/reliable for multi-instance or long-lived authenticated sessions.

4) Default seeded credentials are insecure.
- `ensureAdminUser()` seeds `admin/admin` if missing.
- This is unsafe unless forced password change / disabled in production.

5) Input hardening for event update payload is partial.
- `PATCH /api/events/:eventId` validates formats, but has no strong business-rule checks (e.g., end >= start across mixed all-day/timed edge cases, stricter field limits).

6) No formal schema enforcement layer.
- Native Mongo usage has inferred schema only; no Mongoose/JSON schema validation.
- Risk: document drift or malformed writes under future feature expansion.

7) Observability/testing gaps.
- There are utility test scripts, but no comprehensive automated test suite for OAuth callback, chat filtering, and write flows.
- No robust health/readiness checks for Ollama dependency and graceful fallback policy for production chat quality.

8) Privacy gap in event content handling.
- Full raw Google event objects are stored (`rawEvent`), which can include sensitive metadata.
- Data minimization/redaction policy is not implemented.

END OF REPORT
